Abstract written by a model

In The Wonderful Wizard of Oz, Dorothy is an orphaned Kansas girl who travels through Oz, defeats the Wicked Witch of the West, helps her friends, and returns home. In The Marvelous Land of Oz, Dorothy is a Kansas girl carried to Oz, saved from the Wicked Witch’s bees, and returned to Kansas by Glinda. In Ozma of Oz, Dorothy is a brave Kansas traveler who rescues Ev’s royal family, defeats the Nome King’s challenge, and becomes a Princess of Oz. In Dorothy and the Wizard in Oz, Dorothy is a brave Kansas traveler who survives an underground journey, rescues her companions with the Magic Belt, and returns home. In The Road to Oz, Dorothy is a Kansas girl who travels with Toto and companions to Oz, helps them through dangers, and returns toward Kansas by the Magic Belt. In The Emerald City of Oz, Dorothy is a beloved Kansas girl who becomes a Princess of Oz, lives there with her family, and helps protect Oz from the Nome King. In Sky Island: is_a friend [old, old] Dorothy is described as an old, old friend of Baum's readers. In Little Wizard Stories of Oz, Dorothy is a Kansas girl living in Oz who is captured by the Wizard, made to wash dishes, and then returns angrily to the Emerald City.

and 16 more works

In The Patchwork Girl of Oz, Dorothy is Ozma’s companion who joins Ojo’s search, protects the travelers, helps rescue the Scarecrow, and guides the party through Oz. In Tik-Tok of Oz: Dorothy is a girl and friend of Betsy Bobbin, the Cowardly Lion, and the Hungry Tiger. In The Scarecrow of Oz, Dorothy is Ozma’s constant companion who recognizes Button-Bright, welcomes new friends, and introduces them to Oz and its rulers. In Rinkitink in Oz, Dorothy is a Princess of Oz who joins the Wizard in rescuing Inga’s parents from the Nome King’s caverns and leads them back to Oz. In The Lost Princess of Oz, Dorothy leads the search for Ozma, defeats Ugu with the Magic Belt, rescues her companions, and forgives him. In The Tin Woodman of Oz, Dorothy is a brave Kansas girl who destroys a Wicked Witch, helps rescue the Tin Woodman, and aids Ozma in restoring transformed friends. In The Magic of Oz, Dorothy is Ozma’s friend who seeks a birthday gift, helps recover the Magic Flower, rescues her friends, and ends the magicians’ enchantments. In Glinda of Oz, Dorothy is Ozma’s trusted companion who joins her against the Flatheads and Skeezers and helps survive imprisonment beneath the lake. In The Royal Book of Oz, Dorothy is a Princess of Oz who searches for the missing Scarecrow, rescues her companions, and leads them safely back to Oz. In Kabumpo in Oz, Dorothy is a Princess of Oz who is placed under a spell, explains a knight’s duties, survives an injury, and helps plan an escape. In The Cowardly Lion of Oz, Dorothy is the Cowardly Lion’s faithful friend who joins the effort to restore him after he is petrified. In Grampa in Oz, Dorothy is a Princess of Oz who searches for the missing Princess of Perhaps City, helps Grampa’s army, and solves the prophecy surrounding Tatters. In The Lost King of Oz, Dorothy is a Princess of Oz who rescues Humpy, investigates Mombi’s schemes, and helps restore Ozma and the palace. In The Gnome King of Oz, Dorothy is Ozma’s adviser who captures Ruggedo’s magic belt, counters his mischief, and helps welcome Ozma home. In Jack Pumpkinhead of Oz: Dorothy is a little girl from the United States who lives in Emerald City, where she is Ozma's best friend and adviser and prefers life to America. In The Yellow Knight of Oz: Dorothy is a Princess of Oz from Kansas who lives in Ozma's palace with Ozma and the other girls, advises Ozma on important state matters, and has discovered the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, Tik Tok, and Scraps.

One sentence for each work Dorothy is in, in date order. A model wrote them when it joined the works; each title opens that work’s own account below.

written by a model marks a model’s summary or wording. the source’s own words are the quotations: each one opens the source text at its passage, where it can be read in context and judged.

In each work

Dorothy in each of the 24 accounts the graph joined, in date order. Nothing is merged: an abstract, a narrative and a claim stay with the work they were written from.

Dorothy in The Wonderful Wizard of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1900-04 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is an orphaned child from Kansas who lives with Aunt Em and Uncle Henry and loves her dog Toto. A cyclone carries her house to the Land of Oz, where its landing kills the Wicked Witch of the East. Dorothy receives the Witch’s silver shoes and the protective kiss of the Witch of the North, then follows the yellow brick road to seek the Great Oz’s help in returning home. Her wish to return to Kansas, Aunt Em, and Uncle Henry remains what matters most throughout her journey. She is innocent, small, and meek, but also compassionate and brave: she frees the Scarecrow and Tin Woodman, protects Toto from the Cowardly Lion, welcomes the Lion as a companion, helps her friends through dangers, and cares for them when they are harmed. With Toto, the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, and Lion, she faces hunger, ditches, a river, poppies, and other obstacles before reaching the Emerald City. Oz will help only if she kills the Wicked Witch of the West. Though she does not want to kill anyone, Dorothy travels west, is protected from the Winged Monkeys by the Good Witch’s kiss, endures forced work in the Witch’s castle, secretly feeds the Lion, and melts the Witch with water. She frees the Winkies and helps rescue her friends. After returning to Oz, she waits for his promised help, but misses his balloon while searching for Toto. Still longing for Kansas, Dorothy seeks Glinda in the Land of the South. She travels onward with her loving companions, uses the Golden Cap to summon the Winged Monkeys, gives the Cap to Glinda, and learns to use the Silver Shoes. After saying goodbye to her friends, she returns to Kansas with Toto and tells Aunt Em she is glad to be home again.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

24 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter I

Dorothy is an orphan who lives with Uncle Henry and Aunt Em on the Kansas prairies. She loves Toto, catches him when he falls through the open trap door, and remains inside the house when it is lifted by the cyclone. After becoming less frightened, she goes to bed and falls asleep.

Chapter II

Dorothy awakens after the cyclone lands her house in a beautiful country and meets the Witch of the North and the Munchkins. She learns that her house killed the Wicked Witch of the East, receives the witch’s silver shoes, and is told to go to the City of Emeralds to ask Oz for help. The Witch of the North kisses her forehead to protect her, and Dorothy begins preparing to travel by the road paved with yellow brick.

Chapter III

Dorothy prepares food and water, dresses in her clean gingham, and begins traveling to the Emerald City to ask the Great Oz to send her back to Kansas. She stays overnight with Boq and the Munchkins, then meets the Scarecrow, frees him from his pole, and invites him to join her journey.

Chapter IV

Dorothy travels with the Scarecrow and Toto along the rough yellow brick road. She tells the Scarecrow about Kansas and says that people would rather live in their own homes than in any other country. She enters a cottage in the forest and sleeps on dried leaves.

Chapter V

Dorothy searches for water, finds the rusted Tin Woodman, and frees him by oiling his joints with the oil-can. She agrees to let him join the journey to the Emerald City and worries that the bread is nearly gone.

Chapter VI

Dorothy protects Toto by rushing at the Lion and slapping his nose when he tries to bite the dog. She calls the Lion a coward, then welcomes him into the group when he decides to ask Oz for courage. She continues toward the Emerald City to ask Oz to send her and Toto back to Kansas.

Chapter VII

Dorothy camps under a tree, eats the last of her bread, and later gathers nuts and fruit for food. She crosses the first ditch on the Cowardly Lion’s back, helps cross the tree bridge during the Kalidah attack, and becomes tired enough to ride the Lion again. She dreams of the Emerald City and the good Wizard Oz.

Chapter VIII

Dorothy crosses the river on the raft with Toto in her arms, but the current carries the group away from the road of yellow brick. She later falls asleep among the poppies, and the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman carry her to safety beside the river.

Chapter IX

Dorothy wakes and is astonished to find thousands of field mice around her. She meets the Queen of all the Field Mice, thanks the mice for rescuing the Cowardly Lion, eats fruit brought by the Scarecrow, and holds Toto tightly so he will not chase the Queen.

Chapter X

Dorothy resumes the journey with her companions and asks a farmhouse family for food and a place to sleep. She wants the Great Oz to send her back to Kansas, reaches the Emerald City, and is fitted with locked green spectacles before entering the city.

Chapter XI

Dorothy enters the City of Oz with Toto and her companions and receives a comfortable green room in the Palace of Oz. She asks Oz to send her back to Kansas to Aunt Em and Uncle Henry, but Oz requires her to kill the Wicked Witch of the West first. Although she does not want to kill anyone, she agrees to travel with her friends to confront the Witch.

Chapter XII

Dorothy leaves the Emerald City and travels west with her companions to find the Wicked Witch of the West. The Winged Monkeys carry her safely to the Witch’s castle because the mark of the Good Witch’s kiss protects her. She is forced to work in the castle, secretly feeds the Cowardly Lion, and finally melts the Witch with a bucket of water.

Chapter XIII

Dorothy unlocks the Cowardly Lion's prison, declares the Winkies free, and organizes the rescue of the Tin Woodman and the Scarecrow. She helps care for her friends, thinks of Aunt Em, and leads the decision to return to Oz and claim his promise. Before leaving, she takes the Golden Cap, accepts a diamond-studded bracelet from the Winkies, and starts for the Emerald City.

Chapter XIV

Dorothy travels with the Scarecrow, the Tin Woodman, the Cowardly Lion, and Toto through the great fields, but they lose their way. She summons the Winged Monkeys with the Golden Cap, asks them to carry the group to the Emerald City, and learns the history of their binding.

Chapter XV

Dorothy returns to the Emerald City with the Scarecrow, the Tin Woodman, and the Cowardly Lion after melting the Wicked Witch of the West. She confronts Oz about his promises and asks how she can get back to Kansas. She agrees to keep his secret while hoping he will find a way to send her home.

Chapter XVI

Dorothy tells the Scarecrow that she has always liked him as he was. She watches curiously after Oz gives him his new brains. Oz later recognizes that returning Dorothy to Kansas will require more than imagination.

Chapter XVII

Dorothy longs to return to Kansas and helps Oz sew the silk strips for a balloon. She searches for Toto just before the launch and reaches the balloon too late, so she remains behind when Oz departs.

Chapter XVIII

Dorothy weeps because her hope of returning to Kansas has passed, but she is glad she did not go up in the balloon. She asks the Winged Monkeys to carry her to Kansas, learns that they cannot cross the desert, and decides to seek Glinda's help. She will travel south with the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, and Lion.

Chapter XIX

Dorothy says farewell to the Guardian of the Gate and expresses gratitude for how kindly she was treated in the Emerald City. She travels toward the Land of the South with her companions, hoping to return to Kansas, and helps pick up the Scarecrow after the trees throw him.

Chapter XX

Dorothy sleeps while the Tin Woodman makes a ladder, then climbs over the wall with the others. She apologizes after a china cow breaks its leg and meets a china Princess, whom she offers to carry back to Kansas before learning that the Princess would become stiff and unhappy away from her country. Dorothy helps the group cross the china country carefully.

Chapter XXI

Dorothy travels through the boggy country and forest with her companions. She is briefly afraid when she sees the hundreds of animals, but the Lion explains that they are holding a meeting.

Chapter XXII

Dorothy helps the Scarecrow after he is knocked down and asks what the travelers can do against the Hammer-Heads. She summons the Winged Monkeys, travels with the others to the country of the Quadlings, and is admitted to the Castle of Glinda.

Chapter XXIII

Dorothy tells Glinda the Good Witch about her arrival in the Land of Oz and her adventures with her companions. She gives Glinda the Golden Cap, says goodbye to her friends, and uses the Silver Shoes to return to Kansas.

Chapter XXIV

Dorothy returns from the Land of Oz with Toto. She tells Aunt Em that she is glad to be home again.

Dorothy in The Marvelous Land of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1904-06 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl from Kansas, carried by a cyclone to the Land of Oz. Saved from the Wicked Witch’s Stinging Bees by the Scarecrow, she later returns to Kansas through Glinda the Good and asks for more about the Scarecrow and Tin Woodman.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

1 section; each heading opens the text

The Marvelous Land of Oz

Dorothy is identified as the little girl who made a long journey to see L. Frank Baum and asked him to write more about the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman.

Dorothy in Ozma of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1907 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy Gale is a brave little Kansas girl, Uncle Henry and Aunt Em’s niece, and an experienced traveler with old friends in the Land of Oz. While traveling with Uncle Henry to Australia, she is blown away in a chicken-coop by a cyclone. Calm after the storm, she reaches an unknown shore with a yellow hen she renames Billina, finds a golden key, escapes the Wheelers, and unlocks Tiktok, whose mechanisms she winds and whose service she directs. In the Land of Ev, Dorothy, Billina, and Tiktok reach Princess Langwidere’s palace. Dorothy refuses Langwidere’s demand to exchange heads and is imprisoned in the North Tower, until Ozma and her friends arrive and secure her release. Reunited with the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, Cowardly Lion, and others, she joins Ozma’s expedition to rescue Ev’s royal family from the Nome King. Dorothy speaks politely but firmly to the Nome King, warns Ozma against his dangerous guessing challenge, and, despite fear and losses among her friends, enters the challenge herself. She correctly identifies Prince Evring, transformed into a purple kitten, and restores him. As the rescue continues, Dorothy waits for Billina’s efforts, faces the Nome King’s warriors under the protection of her friends, and takes the Nome King’s jeweled magic belt. Using it, she opens a passage, protects the party, and helps free the Tin Woodman and others. She then returns with Ozma’s party to the Land of Oz, where she is welcomed, given a coronet, and made a Princess of Oz. After happy weeks as Ozma’s guest, she learns that Uncle Henry is ill and lonely in Australia. She gives the magic belt to Ozma, returns to him, and promises to nurse and care for him.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

21 sections; each heading opens the text

front matter .. Author's Note

Dorothy Gale is the subject of the additional adventures requested by Baum’s young readers. The book is described as recording adventures involving her and several familiar characters.

1. The Girl in the Chicken Coop

Dorothy Gale is a little Kansas girl traveling with Uncle Henry to Australia. Worried that he has gone onto the dangerous deck, she searches for him, but the wind carries her away in a chicken-coop; she remains calm, uses the coop as a raft, and falls asleep after the storm quiets.

2. The Yellow Hen

Dorothy Gale awakens in the hen-coop after the storm and speaks with the yellow hen, whom she renames Billina. She reaches the unknown shore, dries her wet shoes and stockings, and finds a golden key after digging it out of the sand.

3. Letters in the Sand

Dorothy reads the words “BEWARE THE WHEELERS!” in the sand and searches the forest for food. She picks and eats a lunch-box, takes a dinner-pail, and is chased by the Wheelers until she and Billina take refuge on a rocky hill.

4. Tiktok the Machine Man

Dorothy follows a hidden path to the top of the rocky hill with Billina, unlocks the rock door with the golden key, and discovers Tiktok inside. She reads the instructions attached to him, winds his thinking, speaking, and action mechanisms, and accepts him as her obedient servant.

5. Dorothy Opens the Dinner Pail

Dorothy discovers that Tiktok has run down, finds his lost clock-key in a crack in the rocks, and rewinds his voice and action machinery. She eats dinner, learns about the Land of Ev and the Land of Oz, and follows Tiktok toward Evna.

6. The Heads of Langwidere

Dorothy travels with Tiktok and Billina into the Land of Ev and helps keep the Wheelers’ weakness secret. At the royal palace, Princess Langwidere offers her head No. 26 in exchange for Dorothy’s head, but Dorothy refuses and is ordered imprisoned.

7. Ozma of Oz to the Rescue

Dorothy Gale is imprisoned in a tower by Princess Langwidere after refusing to exchange her head for one from Langwidere's collection. She sees Ozma of Oz and her friends arrive, calls to them for help, and is released by Langwidere.

8. The Hungry Tiger

Dorothy greets the Scarecrow and Tin Woodman, winds Tiktok so he can bow, and asks about Billina. She reunites with the Cowardly Lion, meets the Hungry Tiger, rescues Billina from the chicken house, and carries her back to the palace.

9. The Royal Family of Ev

Dorothy argues that imprisoning a queen mother and her ten children is wrong and agrees to join Ozma's expedition. She looks after Billina, talks with the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman, meets the Sawhorse, and rides in the golden chariot as the party departs.

10. The Giant with the Hammer

Dorothy explains Billina's daily egg-laying habit and rides the Hungry Tiger beneath the Giant with the Hammer. She later helps with the rescue and continues the journey after the Sawhorse is repaired.

11. The Nome King

Dorothy encourages Ozma to plead with the Nome King and successfully calls him by saying, "PLEASE Mr. Nome King, come here and see us." She argues that the King of Ev did not receive a long life because he drowned, but accepts the Nome King's explanation of the bargain.

12. The Eleven Guesses

Dorothy warns Ozma not to accept the challenge because a wrong guess would enslave her. After Ozma is transformed, Dorothy volunteers to make the next attempt, but the Tin Woodman goes instead.

13. The Nome King Laughs

Dorothy is disheartened by the loss of her friends but eats the cakes and drinks the clay coffee served by the Nome King’s Chief Steward. She worries when Billina is missing and cannot get back into the locked throne room, then goes to sleep.

14. Dorothy Tries to be Brave

Dorothy enters the palace to wind Tiktok and attempt the guessing task. After several failures, she correctly identifies the image of a purple kitten as Evring and restores him, then returns to the throne room with him.

15. Billina Frightens the Nome King

Dorothy correctly guesses one of the Nome King's enchantments and returns safely to the throne room with Prince Evring. She warns Billina that guessing the ornaments is difficult, but Billina enters the enchanted palace anyway.

17. The Scarecrow Wins the Fight

Dorothy waits with Prince Evring for Billina’s restoration mission to succeed and rejoices whenever the bell announces that another enchantment has been broken. She stands protected by the Lion, the Tiger, and the Sawhorse when the Nome King orders his warriors to seize her. At Billina’s urging, she prepares to take the Nome King’s jeweled belt.

18. The Fate of the Tin Woodman

Dorothy takes the Nome King's jeweled magic belt and fastens it around her waist. She commands the belt to open the passage, attempts to restore the Tin Woodman, turns pursuing warriors into eggs, and stops the giant man of iron so the group can pass safely.

19. The King of Ev

Dorothy rides with Evring on the Sawhorse and is pleased when Billina restores the Tin Woodman. She embraces the Tin Woodman and tells him that his enchantment is over.

20. The Emerald City

Dorothy accepts Ozma's invitation to return to the Land of Oz because she is anxious to see it again. She travels with Ozma's party, is welcomed by people who remember her, receives a coronet, and is made a Princess of Oz.

21. Dorothy's Magic Belt

Dorothy spends several happy weeks in the Land of Oz as Ozma's guest. After seeing that Uncle Henry is ill and lonely in Australia, she decides to return to him, gives the magic belt to Ozma, and says farewell to her friends. She is transported to Uncle Henry and promises to nurse and care for him.

Dorothy in Dorothy and the Wizard in Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1908 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a sweet, gentle, innocent little girl from a Kansas farm, a human heroine and traveler whose strongest loyalties are to her home, family, pets, and friends. Beginning in California without Toto but with her kitten Eureka, she carries Eureka, a suit-case, and a parasol to Hugson’s Siding. An earthquake drops her, Zeb, the buggy, and their companions into an inner world. In the Land of the Mangaboos, she denies causing the Rain of Stones, recognizes Oz, and joins Zeb, the Wizard, Jim, Eureka, and the piglets on a dangerous journey through strange lands. Dorothy is brave and protective: she helps pick the Royal Princess, saves piglets from Eureka, enters the Black Pit, refuses fruit that would make her invisible, gives the braided man her blue hair-ribbon, and urges resistance to the Gargoyles. Captured after fighting with her parasol, she escapes in the buggy and helps the group avoid dragonettes. When they are trapped beneath the earth, she signals Princess Ozma through an enchanted picture; Ozma uses the Nome King’s Magic Belt to bring Dorothy and Eureka to Oz, and Dorothy uses the Belt to rescue the others. In Oz, Dorothy happily reunites with old friends, greets the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger, rides in a royal chariot wearing the Magic Belt, and cares deeply for Eureka. Though she rebukes the kitten for attacking others, she defends her when Eureka is accused of eating Ozma’s piglet and is relieved when she is proved innocent. Finally, longing to help Uncle Henry and Aunt Em at the Kansas farm, Dorothy says farewell to her friends and returns to Kansas with Eureka.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

21 sections; each heading opens the text

To My Readers

Dorothy appears again as the heroine of the current book and is described as sweet, gentle, and innocent. Because she is in California while Toto is in Kansas, she begins this adventure with her kitten, Eureka, instead of her dog.

1. The Earthquake

Dorothy arrives at Hugson's Siding carrying a suit-case, a covered bird-cage containing her kitten Eureka, and a parasol. Zebediah takes her toward Hugson's Ranch, but an earthquake opens a crack beneath the buggy and pulls her into a pit, where she briefly loses consciousness.

2. The Glass City

Dorothy survives the fall into the inner world and realizes that she has begun another strange adventure. She discovers that Eureka can talk and that people and animals can walk on the air. The man with the star holds her and her companions responsible for the Rain of Stones, so she goes with him toward the House of the Sorcerer.

3. The Arrival Of The Wizard

Dorothy arrives in the Land of the Mangaboos with Zeb, the horse, and the buggy. She denies causing the Rain of Stones and tells the Sorcerer that an earthquake caused it. She recognizes Oz when he descends in the balloon and watches him defeat Gwig.

4. The Vegetable Kingdom

Dorothy travels with Zeb, the Wizard, Jim, and Eureka through the gardens of the glass city. She learns how Mangaboos grow on bushes and objects that the Royal Princess is already ripe, while the Prince plans to have the strangers destroyed.

5. Dorothy Picks the Princess

Dorothy helps the Wizard pick the Royal Princess before the man with the star returns. She explores the vegetable gardens, eats fruit, protects the tiny piglets from Eureka, and returns with the others to stay in the House of the Sorcerer.

6. The Mangaboos Prove Dangerous

Dorothy says she dislikes the cold, flabby Mangaboos and wishes there were another place to go. She helps the Wizard present his magic and is spared from being thrown into the Garden of the Clinging Vines.

7. Into the Black Pit and Out Again

Dorothy is awakened by Eureka and helps prepare to rescue Jim and the piglets. She enters the Black Pit to avoid the Mangaboos' thorns, accepts the possibility of dying there, and travels through the mountain holding a lantern.

8. The Valley of Voices

Dorothy enters the beautiful valley with Zeb, the Wizard, Jim, Eureka, and the piglets. She watches the piglets eat the strange fruit and later speaks with the invisible people of Voe, who invite her to eat lunch.

9. They Fight the Invisible Bears

Dorothy refuses to eat the dama-fruit because she does not want to become invisible. She reassures Eureka, helps the kitten avoid the fruit, travels across the river using the water-walking plant, and reaches the foot of Pyramid Mountain with the others.

10. The Braided Man of Pyramid Mountain

Dorothy climbs the stairs with the Wizard and observes the Valley of Voe, the Cloud Fairies, and the strange birds outside the mountain. She gives the braided man a blue hair-ribbon, making him very happy, and receives a box of Rustles from him.

11. They Meet the Wooden Gargoyles

Dorothy rebukes Eureka for wanting to eat the nine piglets and explains that the piglets are the Wizard's pets. After the Gargoyles appear, she refuses to retreat down the stairs and urges the others to fight.

12. A Wonderful Escape

Dorothy fights the Gargoyles with her parasol but is captured with the others. In the prison house, she helps prepare for escape, holds Eureka in the buggy, and becomes anxious when the winged buggy wobbles during flight.

13. The Den of the Dragonettes

Dorothy helps escape the Gargoyles and travels with the Wizard and Zeb through the mountain. She speaks with the dragonettes, learns that they eat people but are tied in place, and helps choose a path away from the cavern before their mother returns.

14. Ozma Uses the Magic Belt

Dorothy travels with Zeb, the Wizard, Jim the cab-horse, Eureka, and the nine tiny piglets through passages beneath the earth. When the group becomes trapped, she relies on Princess Ozma's enchanted picture and the Nome King's Magic Belt, signaling Ozma at four o'clock. Ozma transports Dorothy and Eureka to the Land of Oz, after which Dorothy uses the Belt to bring the others safely to her.

15. Old Friends are Reunited

Dorothy is with Princess Ozma when Oz and Zeb arrive and welcomes Zeb into the Princess's presence. She joyfully reunites with the Scarecrow, Billina, and Nick Chopper. She rebukes Eureka for attacking Billina and threatens to send both the kitten and hen home unless they stop quarreling.

16. Jim, The Cab-Horse

Dorothy rises early, greets the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger with affection, and tells them about the earthquake and her recent adventures. In the palace she discovers that the antlered Gump's Head can still talk despite being mounted on the wall. Princess Ozma then greets Dorothy and leads her to breakfast.

17. The Nine Tiny Piglets

Dorothy rides with Princess Ozma in the royal chariot while wearing the Magic Belt. She has wound up Tik-tok for the occasion and previously left him in Ozma’s care.

18. The Trial of Eureka the Kitten

Dorothy becomes distressed when Ozma’s white piglet disappears and defends Eureka against the accusation. She helps bring Eureka before Ozma and fears what will happen if the kitten is found guilty.

19. The Wizard Performs Another Trick

Dorothy objects when the Woggle-Bug suggests a grave punishment for Eureka and worries miserably when the jury recommends death. After Eureka is freed and the lost piglet is recovered, Dorothy hugs the kitten and is delighted that she is innocent.

20. Zeb Returns to the Ranch

Dorothy wants to return home because Uncle Henry and Aunt Em need her. She uses the enchanted picture to see the farm in Kansas, says farewell to her friends in Oz, and is sent to Kansas with Eureka by Ozma.

Dorothy in The Road to Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1909 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little Kansas girl and country girl who lives at Uncle Henry's farm-house and has Toto as her dog. She is also a traveler, a friend of Princess Ozma and many people in Oz, and is called a Princess of Oz and of the Emerald City. She has crossed the desert more than once: a cyclone first carried her across, the Silver Shoes returned her, and she later returns to Kansas by the Magic Belt. Her earlier Oz experiences include defeating a wicked witch with water, taking the Magic Belt from the Nome King, saving Tik-Tok and Billina, and becoming so well loved that Nick Chopper's tin castle holds life-size tin figures of her and Toto. Her journey begins when, while trying to direct the Shaggy Man toward Butterfield, changed crossroads leave her and Toto lost. She follows him down a strange road, meets the lost Button-Bright, and takes responsibility for him despite his inability to say where he belongs. With Toto, the Shaggy Man, Button-Bright, and later Polychrome, she travels through Foxville, Dunkiton, and dangerous country toward Oz and the Emerald City. She repeatedly trusts Ozma to help: to restore Button-Bright after he receives a fox's head, to send Polychrome home, and to help Dorothy return to Kansas. Captured by the Scoodlers, she escapes with the party, crosses the deadly desert in a sand-boat, and recognizes the Truth Pond, which restores her companions. In Oz, Dorothy reunites with Tik-Tok, Billina, Nick Chopper, the Cowardly Lion, and the Hungry Tiger. She is received with honor as Ozma's friend, stays at the Royal Palace, and dresses in a grey gown embroidered with silver. Ozma reveals that she guided Dorothy's route and watched the journey. Dorothy then receives guests for Ozma's birthday celebration, welcomes many visitors, and joins the procession with the Scarecrow. At the end, saddened by Button-Bright and Polychrome's departures, she chooses the Magic Belt over a bubble journey and is set to return to Kansas with Toto.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

23 sections; each heading opens the text

1. The Way to Butterfield

Dorothy tries to show the Shaggy Man the road to Butterfield, but she becomes confused about which road is correct. She and Toto become lost when the familiar crossroads change into many strange roads, and she is unable to find the way home. She follows the Shaggy Man and Toto down the seventh road.

2. Dorothy Meets Button-Bright

Dorothy travels along the seventh road with the Shaggy Man and Toto. She questions Button-Bright about his name, home, origins, and plans, becomes frustrated by his lack of information, and agrees that he should come along with them.

3. A Queer Village

Dorothy travels with Button-Bright, the Shaggy Man, and Toto into Foxville. She is welcomed by King Dox, learns that Foxville is in Fairyland rather than Kansas, and decides not to worry greatly about returning to Uncle Henry's farm immediately.

4. King Dox

Dorothy is startled and upset when King Renard gives Button-Bright a fox's head, and she asks the King to change him back. She comforts Button-Bright and promises that Princess Ozma can restore him. Dorothy attends the foxes' dinner and theater, agrees to ask Ozma to invite King Dox, and then continues the journey.

5. The Rainbow's Daughter

Dorothy walks with Toto, Shaggy Man, and Button-Bright through an uninhabited countryside. She befriends the lost Polychrome, invites her to travel to the Emerald City, and believes Princess Ozma can help send her home.

6. The City Of Beasts

Dorothy eats luncheon and explains that she may eventually reach Oz because she often finds it after becoming lost. She tells Button-Bright about the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman and continues with the others into Dunkiton.

7. The Shaggy Man's Transformation

Dorothy travels through Dunkiton with Button-Bright, Polychrome, the shaggy man, and Toto. She tells King Kik-a-bray that she is trying to reach the Land of Oz and find Ozma so she can return home, and she promises to ask Ozma to invite him to her birthday celebration.

8. The Musicker

Dorothy travels with Button-Bright, the shaggy man, Polychrome, and Toto when they encounter Allegro da Capo. She learns that his breathing produces music, tells him she will mention his wish to attend Ozma's birthday party, and continues toward the Land of Oz without him.

9. Facing the Scoodlers

Dorothy travels with Button-Bright, Polychrome, the Shaggy Man, and Toto along a rough road toward the Emerald City, though she does not know whether it is the right road. She explains that the country is a fairy country and tries to reassure Button-Bright when the Scoodlers threaten to make them soup. She watches as the Scoodlers attack the Shaggy Man.

10. Escaping the Soup-Kettle

Dorothy is captured with the others and taken to the Queen of the Scoodlers. She calls the Queen ugly and escapes from the cave with Toto and the others after Polychrome distracts the guard.

11. Johnny Dooit Does It

Dorothy reaches the grove hungry and tired with the others, recognizes the desert as one she has crossed before, and explains how magic previously carried her across it. She asks Polychrome about magic and urges the Shaggy Man to summon help; she is delighted when Johnny Dooit proposes a sand-boat.

12. The Deadly Desert Crossed

Dorothy rides in the sand-boat with Toto in her arms. She worries that the travelers will become dust if they are spilled in the desert, and after the crash one of her front teeth is loosened. She believes they have crossed the desert and reached some part of the Land of Oz.

13. The Truth Pond

Dorothy identifies the area as the yellow Country of the Winkies and explains the four countries of the Land of Oz. She recognizes the Truth Pond's power to restore Button-Bright and the Shaggy Man, then joins the others in continuing toward the Emerald City.

14. Tik-Tok and Billina

Dorothy is delighted to reunite with Tik-Tok and Billina. She introduces them to her companions, protects Billina from Toto, winds Tik-Tok's speech and thought machinery when it runs down, and looks forward to seeing Nick Chopper and attending Ozma's birthday celebration.

15. The Emperor's Tin Castle

Dorothy is delighted to find a life-size tin statue of herself and a tin figure of Toto at the castle. She and her companions are welcomed by Nick Chopper, and she explains that animals in Oz are treated with as much consideration as people if they behave themselves.

16. Visiting the Pumpkin-Field

Dorothy has Button-Bright wind up Tik-tok's thinking machine, speech, and action mechanisms. She visits Jack Pumpkinhead, reads the stones marking his former pumpkin heads, and learns that he is still alive and farming pumpkins.

17. The Royal Chariot Arrives

Dorothy travels toward the Emerald City and is treated with great respect by Winkie farm people because she is a friend of Princess Ozma. She joyfully greets the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger and introduces them to Polychrome.

18. The Emerald City

Dorothy introduces Button-Bright to the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger and assures him that they are kind and good. She rides in Ozma's royal chariot with Button-Bright and Polychrome, then arrives at the Royal Palace, where she happily greets Jellia Jamb and asks about Ozma, her birthday, and the Scarecrow.

19. The Shaggy Man's Welcome

Dorothy dresses in a grey gown embroidered with silver and goes to the palace drawing-room with Button-Bright and Toto to meet Princess Ozma.

20. Princess Ozma Of Oz

Dorothy joyfully greets Ozma and learns that Ozma guided her toward Oz and watched her journey in the Magic Picture. She presents the shaggy man to Ozma, is upset when he admits that he lied about receiving the Love Magnet from the Eskimo, and says that she does not like the Musicker's music. She hopes that the Scarecrow will return in time for the celebration.

21. Dorothy Receives the Guests

Dorothy invites Polly and the shaggy man to breakfast with her and Button-Bright. At Ozma's request, she receives the invited guests in the Throne-Room, greets the visitors, pets Billina's chicks, opens their lockets, and accepts King John Dough's birthday present for Ozma.

22. Important Arrivals

Dorothy welcomes Santa Claus, the Queen of Merryland, the royal family of Ev, King Renard, Queen Zixi, King Bud, and Princess Fluff to Princess Ozma's celebration. She is told by Santa that he sees her every Christmas Eve and prepares with Button-Bright to dress for the evening banquet.

24. The Birthday Celebration

Dorothy walks arm in arm with the Scarecrow in the birthday procession and takes part in the celebration with her friends. She is sorry when Button-Bright and Polychrome leave, chooses the Magic Belt rather than a bubble journey, and is scheduled to return to Kansas with Toto.

Dorothy in The Emerald City of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1910 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl from Kansas, raised and cared for by her beloved Aunt Em and Uncle Henry on a farm. She is usually loving and sweet-tempered, with a round rosy face and earnest eyes, and is described as simple, sweet, true, honest, and a dreamer. Though young, her adventures give her unusual experience and wisdom. She owns Toto, befriends Billina and many leading people of Oz, and is Ozma’s faithful friend and chosen companion. Her adventures in the Land of Oz make her important there: she captures the Nome King’s Magic Belt, destroys two wicked witches, finds the Scarecrow, and helps rescue Nick Chopper. Because the people love her and credit her with good luck, Ozma makes her a Princess of Oz, gives her rooms in the Emerald City palace, and brings Aunt Em and Uncle Henry from their threatened Kansas farm to live with her in Oz. Dorothy then travels through Oz with her relatives and companions, helping the Cuttenclips and Fuddles and learning the country’s people, customs, and places. When she becomes separated from the party, she is captured in Utensia with Toto and Billina but is released, then visits Bunbury and Bunnybury. In Bunnybury she becomes rabbit-sized, receives a royal welcome, and encourages the unhappy Rabbit King to consider the effect of his discontent on others. She returns safely to her companions and continues the journey. When the Nome King threatens Oz, Dorothy fears for Ozma, her friends, and her family. Although she considers using the Magic Belt to escape to Kansas, she refuses to abandon Oz and is willing to become a slave with its people. The danger ends when the invaders become harmless, and Glinda makes Oz invisible. Dorothy remains permanently in Oz with Aunt Em, Uncle Henry, and Toto, and says that she and Toto will always love the writer and the children who love them.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

26 sections; each heading opens the text

1. How the Nome King Became Angry

Dorothy is described as the little girl who captured or took the Nome King's Magic Belt and carried it away. Roquat intends to destroy or enslave her, but she does not know that he remains a dangerous enemy.

2. How Uncle Henry Got Into Trouble

Dorothy Gale lives on a Kansas farm with Aunt Em and Uncle Henry. She is loving, usually sweet-tempered, and has experienced strange adventures in the Land of Oz. She promises to help her aunt and uncle, then makes a secret signal to Ozma and disappears from her room.

3. How Ozma Granted Dorothy's Request

Dorothy Gale tells Ozma that Uncle Henry and Aunt Em are in serious trouble because they are about to lose their Kansas farm. She asks to live in the Land of Oz with them, and Ozma agrees to bring them there. Ozma also tells Dorothy that she will live in her own rooms in the palace as Ozma's constant companion.

4. How The Nome King Planned Revenge

Dorothy is said to possess the Nome King's Magic Belt. Guph identifies her possession of the Belt as one of the magical obstacles to conquering Oz.

5. How Dorothy Became a Princess

Dorothy receives a joyful welcome in the Emerald City because the people consider her a favorite and believe she has brought them good luck. Princess Ozma appoints her a Princess of Oz, and Dorothy dresses in a sky-blue silk gown, pearl-trimmed shoes, and a pearl coronet. She is especially happy that Uncle Henry and Aunt Em are brought to Oz to live with her.

7. How Aunt Em Conquered the Lion

Dorothy shows Aunt Em and Uncle Henry their rooms, the palace, and the surrounding grounds. She introduces them to Billina and explains where several of her friends live. She leaves Aunt Em and Uncle Henry to explore while she stays with Billina's young chickens.

9. How the Wogglebug Taught Athletics

Dorothy settles into her new home in the Emerald City and becomes concerned that Uncle Henry and Aunt Em have no work to occupy them. She asks Ozma for help and then leads her relatives and their escort on a journey through the Land of Oz. At the Royal Athletic College of Oz, she questions when the students study and how they take the School Pills.

10. How the Cuttenclips Lived

Dorothy travels with the party to the Cuttenclip village and explains that the Cuttenclips are living paper dolls. She carefully helps stand up the paper soldiers and later helps Miss Cuttenclip rescue the dolls scattered by the Shaggy Man’s sneeze.

12. How they Matched the Fuddles

Dorothy leads the travelers toward Fuddlecumjig because Ozma thought they might enjoy visiting the Fuddles. She helps match the cook, Lord High Chigglewitz, and Grandmother Gnit together, and she learns that the Fuddles scatter themselves as a peculiarity.

13. How the General Talked to the King

Dorothy is claimed by Roquat the Red as one of the prisoners he intends to capture. He plans to transform her into a china ornament and remembers that her Yellow Hen helped defeat the Nomes by providing eggs.

14. How the Wizard Practiced Sorcery

Dorothy directs the journey toward the Rigmaroles and agrees to follow the southwest path. At the campsite, she trusts the Wizard's abilities and is asked to watch and cook the magically produced supper.

15. How Dorothy Happened to Get Lost

Dorothy helps judge the zebra and the soft-shell crab's dispute and agrees with the Wizard that there is more water than land in the world. The next morning, she walks into the forest with Billina and Toto, becomes lost, and is captured by the Spoon Brigade.

16. How Dorothy Visited Utensia

Dorothy is escorted into Utensia as a prisoner with Billina and Toto. She questions the utensils’ proposed punishment, asks King Kleaver to decide her fate, and is eventually freed after Judge Sifter finds no charge against her; she then enters the forest to look for the camp and blackberries.

17. How They Came to Bunbury

Dorothy reaches Bunbury while lost, worried, and hungry. She politely asks the inhabitants for food, eats a wheelbarrow, a piano, milk, and other baked items, and tours the town with C. Bunn. After Toto and Billina cause trouble, Dorothy defends them but decides to leave when the inhabitants threaten to bake her.

18. How Ozma Looked into the Magic Picture

Dorothy is watched by Princess Ozma through the Magic Picture while traveling with her companions at Fuddlecumjig. Ozma remembers Dorothy's role in the expedition against the Nome King and that Dorothy carried King Roquat's Magic Belt back to the Land of Oz.

19. How Bunnybury Welcomed the Strangers

Dorothy follows the road to Bunnybury and presents the Keeper of the Wicket with Ozma's letter. She enters after agreeing to leave Billina and Toto outside and to reduce herself to the size of the rabbits, then receives respectful bows from the rabbit inhabitants.

20. How Dorothy Lunched With a King

Dorothy is welcomed into the palace at Bunnybury and has luncheon with the Rabbit King. She listens to his complaints about being King and promises to ask Glinda the Good to replace him. She tours the palace gardens and reception hall and is presented as a guest to the court.

21. How the King Changed His Mind

Princess Dorothy enjoys the rabbit performances but refuses to ask Glinda to let the King keep the Whiskered Friskers or his Bodyguard, and agrees only doubtfully to ask about taking a few jugglers. She tells the King that his unhappiness is making the other rabbits discontented, then leaves Bunnybury with Toto and Billina.

22. How the Wizard Found Dorothy

Dorothy returns to the camp after being lost and tells the others about her adventures in Utensia, Bunbury, and Bunnybury. She resumes the journey with the group and decides afterward to speak more concisely.

23. How They Encountered the Flutterbudgets

Dorothy travels with the Wizard and the other travelers to Flutterbudget Center. She reassures a woman that pricking a finger is harmless, asks whether “ifs” make Flutterbudgets, and recalls finding the rusted Tin Woodman with the Scarecrow. She recognizes the Tin Castle as they approach it.

24. How the Tin Woodman Told the Sad News

Dorothy visits the Tin Woodman with Uncle Henry, Aunt Em, the Wizard, and their companions. She learns that the Nome King is preparing to invade Oz and worries about the danger to Ozma, her friends, and her family.

25. How the Scarecrow Displayed His Wisdom

Dorothy travels with the Tin Woodman, the Wizard, and the others to consult the Scarecrow about Ozma's trouble. She is impressed by the Scarecrow's enormous corn-shaped mansion and considers escaping to Kansas with Ozma by using the Magic Belt.

26. How Ozma Refused to Fight for Her Kingdom

Dorothy worries deeply about Ozma and the threatened kingdom. She suggests using the Magic Belt to send everyone to Kansas, but later decides to remain with Ozma and become a slave rather than abandon the people of Oz.

28. How They Drank at the Forbidden Fountain

Dorothy arrives with Uncle Henry and Aunt Em after a sleepless and anxious night. She and Ozma laugh when the dreaded invaders become harmless after drinking from the Forbidden Fountain.

29. How Glinda Worked a Magic Spell

Dorothy agrees that Glinda may make Oz invisible and asks whether the change will affect its inhabitants. She learns that the people of Oz will still be able to see one another and everything in their country.

30. How the Story of Oz Came to an End

Dorothy Gale sends the writer a note saying that Oz is now cut off forever from the rest of the world. She says that she and Toto will always love the writer and the other children who love them.

Dorothy in Little Wizard Stories of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1913 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a Kansas girl living in the Land of Oz by invitation, often seated at Ozma's feet, and a faithful friend of the Wizard of Oz. Traveling with Toto through southeastern Oz, she is captured by the disguised Wizard and made to wash dishes. Frightened by Crinklink, she learns it was a lesson, becomes angry, and returns to the Emerald City.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

1 section; each heading opens the text

LITTLE DOROTHY AND TOTO

Dorothy travels with Toto through the wild wooded hills at the southeast of Oz despite the Wizard of Oz's warnings. She is captured by the disguised Wizard, ordered to wash dishes, and frightened into believing Crinklink may harm her. After learning that the adventure was a lesson, she is angry at being fooled and decides to return to the Emerald City of Oz.

Dorothy in The Patchwork Girl of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1913-07-01 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy, formerly Dorothy Gale of Kansas, is a little girl who has become a Princess of Oz and lives permanently in the Land of Oz. She lives in the royal palace as Ozma's companion, with Aunt Em and Uncle Henry also in Oz, yet remains simple and unspoiled despite the palace's magnificence. She wears an emerald-green hair-ribbon with a plain white frock, and plain gingham dress and checked sunbonnet when traveling. Ozma is her very best friend, and Dorothy knows Oz fairly well through earlier discoveries of the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, Cowardly Lion, and Tik-Tok the Clockwork Man. At the outset, Dorothy helps provide news of Oz after learning by wireless communication that the writer wants it. She sympathizes with Ojo's troubles, listens carefully to accounts involving Ojo, Scraps, the Glass Cat, and the Woozy, and gives the Woozy and Glass Cat rooms while keeping Scraps in one of her own. She is fond of the Patchwork Girl for being queer and uncommon, trusts the Woozy as good, honest, and faithful, and is less fond of the Glass Cat. Dorothy chooses to join Ojo's search for the magical ingredients, including water from a dark well. As an experienced traveler, she brings a gold flask, travels through unsettled parts of Oz, fights the Tottenhots to rescue her companions, helps the group rest, carries Toto during danger, and refuses to let Ojo climb the mountain alone. She finds the concealed tunnel to the dark well, captures Hip Hopper with Toto's help, and responds to the war between the Hoppers and Horners by offering the Champion liberty and advising the Hoppers to laugh at the Horners' poor joke. She helps rescue and repair the Scarecrow, gives Ojo the flask for the well water, leads the travelers toward the Winkie Country, assists with a raft and river crossing, and calms an angry wife with a promise of repayment. At the Tin Woodman's castle, she supports sparing a butterfly, reports the failure to obtain its wing to Ozma, and reassures Ojo that no one knows what will happen next.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

14 sections; each heading opens the text

The Patchwork Girl of Oz / Prologue

Dorothy Gale, afterward Princess Dorothy of Oz, helped the writer become Royal Historian of Oz. She learned through wireless communication that he wanted news from Oz and asked Ozma for permission to provide it.

Chapter Fourteen

Dorothy is described as a girl who came to Oz from Kansas, where she was Dorothy Gale. She is now a Princess, lives in Oz with Aunt Em and Uncle Henry, and is expected to sympathize with Ojo and speak to Ozma about his troubles.

Chapter Sixteen

Dorothy Gale lives in the royal palace as a Princess of Oz but remains simple and unspoiled. She questions Scraps and the Glass Cat, listens to the Shaggy Man's account of Ojo, and recognizes the Woozy's willingness to help. She arranges rooms for the Woozy and the Glass Cat and keeps Scraps in one of her own rooms.

Chapter Seventeen

Dorothy attends Ozma's banquet and says that she has taken a fancy to the Patchwork Girl because she is queer and uncommon. She believes the Woozy is good, honest, and faithful, but says she does not care much for the Glass Cat.

Chapter Eighteen

Dorothy attends Ojo's trial and asks permission to accompany him. She agrees to help search for the magical ingredients and says that she, Ojo, and the others will probably have to look in unsettled parts of Oz for a dark well.

Chapter Nineteen

Dorothy visits Jack Pumpkinhead and helps clarify that a gold flask she carries holds more than a gill. She travels with the group toward the Quadling Country, fights the Tottenhots when they attack her companions, and arranges for the travelers to sleep in one of their houses.

Chapter Twenty

Dorothy asks about a dark well and travels with the others toward the rocky mountains. She helps carry Toto while escaping past Mister Yoop and is knocked down when the Scarecrow is thrown at Ojo and her.

Chapter Twenty-One

Dorothy climbs the mountain with Ojo, Scarecrow, Scraps, and Toto while searching for the dark well. She refuses to let Ojo climb alone, discovers that a rock conceals the tunnel entrance, and captures Hip Hopper with Toto's help. She learns that the Hoppers and Horners are separate nations and that they are at war.

Chapter Twenty-Two

Dorothy offers to give the Champion his liberty so he can help the Hoppers. After the Scarecrow becomes trapped on the fence, she reassures Ojo that they will rescue him.

Chapter Twenty-Three

Dorothy explains to the Hoppers that the Horners' joke is poor and advises them to laugh at it so the conflict will end. She helps pull the Scarecrow from the fence and sews the hole in his back. She later examines the Scarecrow and finds that one of his legs is ripped.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Dorothy follows Diksey into the mine with Ojo and the others. She gives Ojo the gold flask so he can fill it with water from the dark well.

Chapter Twenty-Five

Dorothy leads the travelers toward the Country of the Winkies so Ojo can find a yellow butterfly, hoping to reach the Tin Woodman's castle by a crosscut. She asks the Quadling man how to cross the river, offers him an emerald ring, helps build the raft, and calms his angry wife by promising repayment.

Chapter Twenty-Six

Dorothy travels with the others on the raft toward the Winkie Country and suggests reaching land when the Trick River carries them the wrong way. She explains that the Tin Woodman is kind-hearted and dislikes seeing living things hurt, including flowers and insects. At the castle, she helps raise the Scarecrow so he can look for the Tin Woodman's home and later helps urge the raft toward shore.

Chapter Twenty-Seven

Dorothy helps tell the Tin Woodman how Scraps was made and describes the party's adventures. She recognizes that the Tin Woodman is right to oppose harming a butterfly and later tells Ozma about the failure to obtain its wing. She assures Ojo that no one knows what will happen next.

Dorothy in Tik-Tok of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1914 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a girl and friend of Betsy Bobbin, the Cowardly Lion, and the Hungry Tiger. She owns Toto and possesses a silver whistle. She urges Ozma to welcome Betsy and Hank and save Shaggy Man’s group, warmly greets them, and delights when Toto finally speaks before running away.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

2 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Twenty-Four

Dorothy advises Ozma to admit Betsy and Hank to the Land of Oz and says that Shaggy Man's group should not be lost. She eagerly runs to welcome Betsy when the girl and Hank arrive in the palace.

Chapter Twenty-Five

Dorothy is defended by the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger as the sweetest girl in the world. She tests whether Toto can speak words, learns that he prefers not to, and is delighted when he finally says, "All right. Here I go!" before running away.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl and Princess of Oz who formerly lived in Kansas and now lives at Ozma's palace in Emerald City. Learning of Inga's rescue attempt, she gains permission to help, travels with the Wizard of Oz to the Nome King's caverns carrying eggs, forces Kaliko to release Inga's parents, and leads everyone back to Oz.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

2 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Twenty

Dorothy learns about Inga's rescue attempt through Glinda's Great Book of Records and later watches his adventures with Ozma's Magic Picture. She asks Ozma for permission to help Inga and travels with the Wizard of Oz to the Nome Country, carrying eggs as protection against the nomes.

Chapter Twenty-One

Dorothy arrives with the Wizard of Oz, warmly greets Inga, and demands that Kaliko release King Kitticut and Queen Garee. She compels Kaliko to obey by threatening to show him eggs, then leads the group back to the Land of Oz.

Dorothy in The Lost Princess of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1917 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a mortal Earth and Kansas girl who lives in a delightful suite in Ozma’s royal palace as a Princess of Oz and Ozma’s dearest, devoted friend. When Ozma disappears, Dorothy searches the palace, helps use the Magic Picture, and becomes deeply determined to find her, convinced that a wicked person is responsible. She leads and joins a traveling search party with Betsy, Trot, the Wizard, Toto, and other companions through the Winkie Country and toward Ugu the Shoemaker’s castle. Loyal to Ozma as ruler, she repeatedly defends Ozma’s authority, refuses unsuitable food and offers, protects and scolds her companions, and persists through dangers including mountains, thistles, traps, and hostile-looking soldiers. Wearing the Magic Belt, she is protected during the journey and later secretly learns its powers. She uses it to reverse a rotating-room trap, freeing her companions, then confronts Ugu and transforms him into a dove, defeating the magician who stole Ozma and magic from the Emerald City. After Ozma is released, Dorothy embraces her, recounts the victory, and keeps Ugu transformed until he reforms. When Ugu expresses sorrow, Dorothy forgives him, saying that anyone who is sorry has to be forgiven.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

18 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 1

Dorothy lives in a suite of rooms in Ozma’s royal palace and is Ozma’s dearest friend. She discovers that Ozma is missing, searches throughout the palace and its grounds, questions several people, and helps try to locate Ozma with the Magic Picture.

CHAPTER 5

Dorothy is deeply worried that Ozma has been abducted and insists that a wicked person in the Land of Oz must be responsible. She agrees to lead a search party into the Winkie Country, with Betsy, Trot, and the Wizard of Oz traveling with her.

CHAPTER 6

Dorothy joins the Wizard’s search party and travels in the Red Wagon through the Winkie Country. She accepts Toto into the party after discovering that he has followed them, and she insists that they continue searching despite the dangers ahead.

CHAPTER 7

Dorothy recognizes the Merry-Go-Round Mountains and helps consider ways to cross them. She wears the Magic Belt, which protects her from harm, and crosses by swinging on the strap while holding Toto; after several bumps, she lands safely in a meadow.

CHAPTER 8

Dorothy travels toward the west with the group and rides on the Cowardly Lion. She helps devise a way to carry the Lion and Hank across the thistles and is present when the Patchwork Girl walks through the city's wall.

CHAPTER 9

Dorothy asks whether the Thists have a King and later explains that Royal Ozma rules over their city as part of Oz. She refuses the offered thistle food because the travelers' insides are not lined with gold and urges the group to leave.

CHAPTER 11

Dorothy worries when Button-Bright disappears and later scolds him for wandering away while the group is searching for Ozma. She tells Toto that his lost growl may return and agrees that stealing Ozma is a serious wrongdoing.

CHAPTER 12

Dorothy travels to the City of Herku with the others and objects when Vig the Czarover is presented as a ruler greater than Ozma. She questions the Czarover about the strength of the people of Herku and rejects his offer of zosozo because she does not want to become thin.

CHAPTER 17

Dorothy is amazed by the Frogman and welcomes the Frogman's party. She recognizes the immense book Ugu was examining as Glinda's Great Book of Records and concludes that Ugu stole Ozma and the magic in the Emerald City.

CHAPTER 18

Dorothy asks what Ugu has done with Ozma and is horrified to learn that Ozma may be in a dungeon cell. She suggests that she and Scraps go on alone, but agrees that the travelers should approach Ugu together and inspect his castle.

CHAPTER 19

Dorothy joins the Wizard of Oz in an expedition to find Ugu the Shoemaker and force him to surrender the magical possessions he stole.

CHAPTER 20

Dorothy travels with the group toward Ugu’s castle and helps lead the search for Ozma. She questions the little Pink Bear, apologizes to the Bear King when its answers seem false, helps rescue Button-Bright, and considers whether Ozma might have been transformed or made invisible.

CHAPTER 21

Dorothy travels with the group toward Ugu the Shoemaker’s wicker castle and asks how the steel wall can be overcome. She and the others eventually pass through the apparently threatening army of girl-soldiers.

CHAPTER 22

Dorothy secretly experiments with the Magic Belt and discovers that it can perform transformations and grant its wearer one wish a day. She uses that wish to make the hall revolve back into its proper position, freeing her companions from the rotating-room trap. She and the others then help Scraps after Scraps falls from the chandelier.

CHAPTER 23

Dorothy confronts Ugu the Shoemaker and uses the Magic Belt to transform him into a dove. She later makes the dove as small as a canary bird, and the others credit her with defeating the wicked magician.

CHAPTER 24

Dorothy suggests asking the little Pink Bear where Ozma is and later asks Button-Bright to empty his pocket. She rushes forward and embraces Ozma after Ozma is released.

CHAPTER 25

Dorothy helps tell Ozma how the party conquered Ugu and explains that she will keep Ugu in the form of a dove until he reforms and becomes a good, honest shoemaker.

CHAPTER 26

Dorothy listens to Ugu’s request for forgiveness and offers to use her Magic Belt to transform him back into his regular shape. When Ugu refuses, she forgives him and tells him that anyone who is sorry has to be forgiven.

Dorothy in The Tin Woodman of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1918 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little, brave, true, adventurous Kansas girl, a princess of Oz, and Ozma's close friend, living with Ozma and caring for her faithful little dog Toto. A cyclone carries her house from Kansas to the Land of Oz, where it destroys the Wicked Witch in Munchkin Country. She takes the Silver Shoes to the Emerald City and, with the Scarecrow, rescues the rusted Tin Woodman. Later she travels with Ozma to aid transformed friends, rejoices when Ozma restores the Scarecrow and Tin Woodman, pities Woot, protects Toto, and plans to help prepare wedding festivities.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

4 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Two

Dorothy arrives in the Land of Oz after a cyclone carries her house from Kansas. When the house falls in the Munchkin Country, it lands on and destroys the Wicked Witch, and Dorothy later rescues the rusted Tin Woodman with the Scarecrow.

Chapter Twelve

Dorothy is Ozma's close friend and watches her friends' activities through the Magic Picture. She becomes alarmed by their transformations and travels with Ozma in the Red Wagon to try to help them.

Chapter Thirteen

Dorothy arrives with Ozma and helps learn what happened to the transformed characters. She is relieved when Ozma restores the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman, feels pity for Woot, and refuses to sacrifice Toto's form to restore him.

Chapter Fifteen

Dorothy questions whether the Tin Woodman loves Nimmie Amee and whether Nimmie Amee still loves him. She wants to join the search but does not ask because the Tin Woodman does not invite her, and she plans instead to help Ozma prepare the wedding festivities.

Dorothy in The Magic of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1919 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a fearless Kansas girl and a Princess of Oz, though she prefers simply to be called Dorothy and does not like the princess title. Ozma of Oz's dearest friend, she lives in luxurious rooms in the Royal Palace, frequently discovers hidden places in Oz, and is an old friend of the Wizard of Oz as well as a friend and companion of Trot and Cap'n Bill. Her story centers on loyalty, practical courage, and her wish to give Ozma a birthday gift. Worried that Ozma already has everything, she consults friends, seeks the Magic Flower, and joins the Wizard in obtaining and training twelve tiny dancing monkeys for a surprise cake they will give together. During these efforts, Dorothy defends the Cowardly Lion's courage, but Kiki transforms her into a lamb. She dislikes the form and wants to be as she was born; the Wizard restores her natural shape. She then helps search for his black bag, rescue giant soldiers, and travel with the Wizard, the Glass Cat, and caged monkeys. Riding the Cowardly Lion, she heads toward the Magic Isle to save Trot and Cap'n Bill. Alarmed that the captives may shrink away, she proposes seeking Glinda when needed and later suggests turning them into bumblebees, which helps them survive. Dorothy supports retrieving the Magic Flower, rides the Hungry Tiger with Trot while carrying the monkeys' cage, and cares for the injured Glass Cat by washing its eyes and ears. After the flower reaches the Royal Palace, she attends Ozma's feast beside the Scarecrow, presents the Surprise Cake, and delights in the monkeys' performance. At the end, she proposes using the Fountain of the Water of Oblivion so the transformed magicians forget their magic, helps Kiki Aru and Ruggedo drink it, and kindly seats the memoryless Kiki beside her.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

13 sections; each heading opens the text

5. A Happy Corner of Oz

Dorothy is a Kansas girl, a Princess of Oz, and Ozma of Oz's dearest friend, though she prefers to be called simply Dorothy. She wants to give Ozma a birthday present and helps persuade the Glass Cat to guide her companions to the Magic Flower.

6. Ozma's Birthday Presents

Dorothy worries about finding Ozma a birthday present that she does not already have. After consulting Scraps, the Tin Woodman, the Scarecrow, Toto, and Glinda the Good, she decides to make Ozma a surprise cake containing tiny dancing monkeys. She asks the Wizard of Oz to help her find and train the monkeys and agrees to make the cake a present from both of them.

12. Kiki Uses His Magic

Dorothy tells Gugu that the Cowardly Lion is cowardly but not a coward, because he fights when necessary and cannot be conquered by any living beast. Kiki transforms her into a lamb.

13. The Loss of the Black Bag

Dorothy is transformed into a Lamb and dislikes being in that form, saying she would rather be as she was born. She helps search for the Wizard's black bag and suggests finding the Goose because he may be angry at the Magician and able to help.

14. The Wizard Learns the Magic Word

Dorothy has been transformed into a Lamb and is restored to her natural shape by the Wizard of Oz. She is delighted when the Wizard returns and asks what happened to the giant soldiers.

16. The Glass Cat Finds the Black Bag

Dorothy accompanies the Wizard during the rescue of the six giant soldiers and the selection of the twelve monkeys. She rides the Cowardly Lion and travels toward the Emerald City with the Wizard, the Glass Cat, and the caged monkeys.

17. A Remarkable Journey

Dorothy travels with the Wizard and the others toward the Magic Isle to rescue Trot and Cap'n Bill. She asks questions about the isle, the Magic Plant, and the Lonesome Duck, and becomes anxious when she learns that the rooted captives may grow smaller and disappear.

18. The Magic of the Wizard

Dorothy suggests going to Glinda when the Wizard's efforts fail and accompanies him across the river on the raft. She is shocked by how small Trot and Cap'n Bill have become and listens as Trot asks her to explain their fate to Ozma.

19. Dorothy and the Bumble Bees

Dorothy is distressed when Trot and Cap'n Bill become trapped and suggests that the Wizard transform them into bumblebees. She is relieved when they survive and later supports Cap'n Bill's plan to retrieve the Magic Flower for Ozma.

20. The Monkeys Have Trouble

Dorothy helps arrange for the Cowardly Lion to carry the Magic Flower and rides the Hungry Tiger with Trot while carrying the monkeys' cage. She is horrified by the Glass Cat's condition, washes the cat's eyes and ears, and agrees that the punishment is probably enough.

21. The College of Athletic Arts

Dorothy arrived at the Royal Palace with her friends. She was among the friends who knew that the Magic Flower had been brought safely to the palace.

22. Ozma's Birthday Party

Dorothy attends Ozma's birthday feast and sits next to the Scarecrow of Oz. She gives Ozma the Surprise Cake, urges Ozma to cut it, and is delighted when the twelve monkeys emerge and perform.

23. The Fountain of Oblivion

Dorothy suggests using the Fountain of the Water of Oblivion to make the transformed magicians forget all the magic they know. She helps restore Kiki Aru and Ruggedo by arranging for them to drink the water, and she leads the memoryless Kiki Aru to a seat beside her.

Dorothy in Glinda of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1920 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a kindly, innocent mortal girl from Kansas who lives in the Land of Oz as Princess Ozma’s trusted friend and companion. She resides in Ozma’s palace with Trot and Betsy Bobbin, possesses the Nome King’s protective Magic Belt and a Magic Ring given by Glinda, and is noted for winning hearts. The records follow her journey with Ozma to prevent war between the Skeezers and Flatheads. After learning of the conflict in Glinda’s Great Book of Records, Dorothy insists on joining Ozma, equips herself with protective magic, and helps overcome obstacles including a purple web, mist, and the defenses of Flathead Mountain. She questions and challenges the Flatheads, escapes with Ozma through invisibility magic, and reaches the Skeezer island. There she defends Ozma’s authority and is imprisoned with her beneath the lake in the Great Dome, where Lady Aurex cares for them. Dorothy remains brave despite anxiety, thinks through ways to reverse magical transformations, signals danger with her ring, and recognizes rescuers arriving by submarine. When rescue reaches the submerged village, she joyfully reunites with the Wizard and others. At the end, she discovers a hidden drawer with gray powder and identifies Coo, ee, and oh as the three magic words governing its mechanisms; she sees that one releases boats and another raises the island.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

17 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter One

Dorothy discovers the report of war between the Skeezers and Flatheads in Glinda's Great Book of Records. She insists on accompanying Ozma to help prevent the war and receives a protective alarm ring from Glinda; she also plans to wear the Magic Belt she took from the Nome King.

Chapter Two

Dorothy travels with Ozma toward the Skeezer Country while wearing the Nome King's Magic Belt and carrying Glinda's Magic Ring. She discovers that a crab can cut the purple web, carries the crab to the web, and escapes with Ozma.

Chapter Three

Dorothy worries that the dense mist may cause them to get lost or step on something dreadful. She follows Ozma into the mist when the Mist Maidens come, is carried safely across, and thanks them.

Chapter Four

Dorothy travels with Ozma across a broad plain toward the flat mountain where the Flatheads live. She becomes tired from the journey, enjoys the magical feast and comfortable bed, and wishes that everyone could use magic to avoid work and worry.

Chapter Five

Dorothy travels with Ozma toward the flat mountain and asks whether magic can provide a faster means of transportation. She helps search for a way through the mountain’s defenses, climbs the stairway, and becomes concerned when she learns that the Flatheads have been warned of their arrival.

Chapter Six

Dorothy accompanies Princess Ozma into Flathead Mountain and questions the Su-dic about Flathead brains and Rora's transformation. She challenges the Su-dic's claims and vanishes with Ozma before the Flatheads can imprison them.

Chapter Seven

Dorothy escapes with Ozma after Ozma makes them invisible, and she runs with her to safety. She agrees that they should find the Skeezers and hopes the Flatheads will be punished. She discovers the Lake of the Skeezers and the island palace with Ozma.

Chapter Eight

Princess Dorothy crosses to Skeezer island with Princess Ozma and observes that the Skeezers appear unhappy despite their comfortable homes and ample food. She defends Ozma's authority, insults Queen Coo-ee-oh's pride, and is placed under the same imprisonment order as Ozma.

Chapter Nine

Dorothy defends Ozma's identity and declares that Ozma should remove the magic from Queen Coo-ee-oh and Su-dic and provide new rulers. She listens indignantly to Lady Aurex's account of Coo-ee-oh's cruelty and believes the Flatheads may attack the glass dome with bows and arrows.

Chapter Ten

Dorothy remains a prisoner with Ozma in Lady Aurex's house but is treated considerately. She asks about the island's submergence and watches as a submarine approaches the Flatheads.

Chapter Twelve

Dorothy is glad that Coo-ee-oh has been punished but soon understands that the transformation has made escape and rescue more difficult. She suggests capturing the three transformed fishes so Ozma can restore the Adept Witches, then begins watching the lake for them.

Chapter Thirteen

Dorothy travels with Ozma to the Magic Isle of the Skeezers and uses the ring Glinda gave her to signal danger. She is imprisoned with Ozma in the great Dome of the Isle of the Skeezers beneath the lake.

Chapter Fourteen

Dorothy is in danger with Ozma, and Glinda tells the council about their plight. The council resolves to rescue both Dorothy and Ozma.

Chapter Seventeen

Dorothy remains a prisoner with Ozma and the Skeezers beneath the Great Dome. Uncle Henry is especially distressed that she is under water, and the rescuers seek a way to bring her safely out.

Chapter Twenty

Dorothy is imprisoned inside the Great Dome with Ozma but appears safe. She recognizes Glinda and the Wizard through the submarine's windows and exchanges signals with them, though she seems somewhat anxious while trying to remain brave.

Chapter Twenty-Two

Dorothy is with Ozma in the submerged village and is filled with joy when the Wizard and the others arrive. The Wizard embraces her after reaching the ground.

Chapter Twenty-Three

Dorothy discovers a hidden drawer containing moving gray powder and suggests that Coo-ee-oh divided her name into the three magic words Coo, ee, and oh. She observes that Coo releases the boats and later sees that oh has raised the island.

Claims worded by a model

22 claims from 28 quoted facts, in order of appearance. The arrow opens the text at the passage; a claim opens to every quotation behind it.
