Dorothy in The Royal Book of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1921 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a real human little girl from Kansas who is also a Princess of Oz, a resident of Ozma's palace, an adventurer, and the Scarecrow's close friend. She is described as having discovered him at a Munchkin farm and as caring deeply about him, even becoming distressed when he may no longer be the Scarecrow in Oz. At Ozma's party, she hosts blind-man's-buff, catches him, and kindly dismisses worries about his ancestry. When the Scarecrow disappears, Dorothy searches for him and sets out with the Cowardly Lion. After being arrested in Pokes for moving too quickly, she meets Sir Hokus, accepts him as her knight, and escapes Pokes with him and the Lion by singing "Three Blind Mice." Their journey toward the Emerald City and the Scarecrow brings dangers and detours: Dorothy faces a storm, Bangladore, Fix City, a broken road, the Winkie Country, and the Deadly Desert. She is repeatedly rescued or assisted by companions, but she also encourages them, negotiates for the group, prevents Sir Hokus from entering the desert, and takes responsibility for their direction. Her wishes carry the company to Silver Island, where she reunites with and embraces the Scarecrow, learns he is Chang Wang Woe, and insists on returning for the Cowardly Lion when he is left behind. Given a magic fan and parasol, Dorothy becomes central to the escape: she accidentally flies with the parasol, helps end the crisis involving the Silver Princes, devises the return to Oz, and skillfully steers the parasol through a black passage and the beanstalk. Back in Oz, she follows the Scarecrow to rescue Sir Hokus, uses the Magic Belt to recover her fallen fan and bring airborne animals down safely, then tells Ozma the story and gives Ozma the magic fan and parasol. Her story unfolds as a determined search for her friend, ending in a safe return to Oz and the Emerald City.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

18 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 1

Dorothy is hosting the game of blind-man's-buff at Ozma's party and catches the Scarecrow. She reassures the upset Scarecrow about his lack of royal ancestry and suggests that the Professor's own family tree may have been a milkweed. The Professor records her as Dorothy, Princess of Oz and sixth cousin to a President, but she denies being a sixth cousin to a President.

CHAPTER 2

Dorothy previously discovered the Scarecrow at the Munchkin farm. The Scarecrow remembers Dorothy while making a riddle and intends to tell her about it.

CHAPTER 4

Dorothy worries about the Scarecrow after he disappears and searches the palace and gardens for him. She travels with the Cowardly Lion to visit him, survives a storm and a wrong turn, and falls asleep at the gates of the Great Grand and Mighty Slow Kingdom of Pokes.

CHAPTER 5

Dorothy enters Pokes with the Cowardly Lion and is arrested for speeding because she cannot move slowly enough. She meets Sir Hokus, tells him about Oz and the Scarecrow's disappearance, accepts him as her knight, and prepares to leave with him and the Cowardly Lion on their quest.

CHAPTER 6

Dorothy escapes from Pokes by singing "Three Blind Mice" with Sir Hokus and the Cowardly Lion. She stops to watch the Pokes play croquet, later accepts hokey pokey from a Poke, yawns, and falls asleep. After the Cowardly Lion rescues her, she comforts Sir Hokus, fastens his plume to her dress, and helps lead the group toward the Emerald City.

CHAPTER 10

Dorothy is staying at the Scarecrow's and is presumed by Princess Ozma and Betsy Bobbin to be having a lovely time. Princess Ozma does not look in the Magic Picture to see what adventures Dorothy is really having.

CHAPTER 11

Dorothy becomes discouraged while following the narrowing road through the forest, then is frightened when Bangladore approaches. She helps Sir Hokus after his helmet is jammed down, discovers that Bangladore is made of candy, and negotiates their release in exchange for secrecy and the giant's vest buttons.

CHAPTER 12

Dorothy travels with Sir Hokus and the Cowardly Lion toward the Emerald City, telling Sir Hokus about Oz and the Scarecrow. In Fix City, she meets animated furniture and unusual inhabitants, receives dinner and a bed, and goes to sleep while thinking about telling the Scarecrow and Ozma what happened.

CHAPTER 13

Dorothy leaves Fix City with Sir Hokus and the Cowardly Lion after buying a five-mile road. She falls into a stream when the road ends, is rescued by the Cowardly Lion, and later encourages him to accept his new status as a knight.

CHAPTER 16

Dorothy travels with Sir Hokus and the Cowardly Lion through the Winkie Country while searching for the road back to the Emerald City and seeking the Scarecrow. She realizes they have been going in the wrong direction, prevents Sir Hokus from stepping onto the Deadly Desert, and helps arrange their journey with the Comfortable Camel and Doubtful Dromedary.

CHAPTER 17

Dorothy notices that the Doubtful Dromedary has disappeared and witnesses Sir Hokus, the Comfortable Camel, and the Doubtful Dromedary undergo transformations. She wishes Sir Hokus back, then accidentally makes everyone unable to speak by wishing that they would stop wishing. After thinking carefully, she makes an eight-word wish that causes the earth to swallow the whole company.

CHAPTER 18

Dorothy arrives on Silver Island with the Cowardly Lion, Sir Hokus of Pokes, the Comfortable Camel, and the Doubtful Dromedary after wishing to be with the Scarecrow. She finds and embraces the Scarecrow, learns that he is actually Chang Wang Woe, and becomes distressed that he may no longer be the Scarecrow in Oz. She joins the others in climbing the bean pole toward Oz.

CHAPTER 19

Dorothy realizes that the Cowardly Lion has been left behind and insists that the group return for him. She listens to the Scarecrow's plan to abdicate and return to Oz, receives the magic fan and parasol, and keeps the fan in her dress pocket and the parasol on her arm.

CHAPTER 20

Dorothy attends the Ceremony of Restoration and supports the Scarecrow as he tries to avoid being transformed. She screams and raises her hands when the Grand Gheewizard threatens him, accidentally opening the magic parasol and sailing through the broken skylight; she later returns on the parasol and knocks the silver vase onto the three Silver Princes.

CHAPTER 21

Dorothy explains that she closed the magic parasol to descend after flying upward, striking the vase and helping resolve the crisis. She devises the plan for returning to Oz, helps install Happy Toko as Emperor, and opens the parasol so the party can escape the Silver Island.

CHAPTER 22

Dorothy steers the parasol through the deep, black passage with the skill of an aviator. She keeps one hand on the bean pole to hold the parasol in the center, and after it crashes and partly closes, she grasps the pole and snaps the parasol open.

CHAPTER 23

Dorothy helps lower and raise the parasol as the party escapes through the beanstalk and lands safely on a haystack in Oz. She plans to tell Ozma about her adventures and go straight to the Emerald City, but later follows Sir Hokus upward with the Scarecrow when the green branch carries him away.

CHAPTER 24

Dorothy follows the growing beanstalk with the Scarecrow and rescues Sir Hokus by helping hold him down and guiding him toward the Emerald City. She discovers that the magic fan has fallen from her pocket and uses the Magic Belt to wish it closed and returned to her, bringing the three airborne animals safely down. She tells Ozma the story of the Scarecrow's family tree and presents Ozma with the magic fan and parasol.

Dorothy in Kabumpo in Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1922 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy, a Princess of Oz from Kansas who makes her home in Oz after several visits, discovers and favors Scarecrow. She is placed asleep under a strange spell, later explains a knight’s duties and warns of Mr. Yoop, then is injured when Ruggedo’s upheaval shakes the palace. Revived, she recalls eggs endanger gnomes and helps plan escape.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

2 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter 7

Dorothy explains that Sir Hokus's duty as a knight is to slay giants and dragons and go on quests. She tells the group about Mr. Yoop, listens to the giant stories, and becomes uneasy when she hears strange rumbles before the royal palace is shaken.

Chapter 14

Dorothy is knocked unconscious by the upheaval and has an ugly bruise on her forehead. After Ozma and the others revive her, she learns that Ruggedo has become a giant and remembers that eggs are dangerous to gnomes. She asks what the group should do and helps consider how to escape.

Dorothy in The Cowardly Lion of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1923 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl from Kansas, a celebrity in the Emerald City, and an old, faithful friend of the Cowardly Lion. Though Notta hopes she can help return him to the United States, she prefers Oz to returning to Kansas. Bob Up finds her reading in the Emerald City gardens; she mistakes Notta for a witch, drenches him, and calls for help. She later joins the party at Mudge, mourns the petrified Lion, and her tears help restore him to life.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

4 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter 5

Dorothy is believed by Bob Up and Notta to have been liked by the Cowardly Lion and to have come from Kansas. Notta hopes to find her in the Emerald City and ask her to help them return to the United States.

Chapter 18

Dorothy is found by Bob Up reading beside a fountain in the Emerald City gardens. She mistakes Notta for a witch, throws two buckets of water over him, and summons help with a silver whistle.

Chapter 20

Dorothy arrives at the enclosure with the company from the Emerald City. She screams when Mustafa's lions rush toward the Cowardly Lion.

Chapter 21

Dorothy cries when Crunch carries away the petrified Cowardly Lion and later climbs onto the stone figure's back. Her tears begin restoring the Cowardly Lion, and she rejoices when he comes back to life.

Dorothy in Grampa in Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1924 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl from Kansas and the United States who is a Princess, Princess Royal, and Royal Princess of Oz. Though honored and adventurous, she remains jolly and unchanged. She lives in the Emerald City and resides in the Emerald Palace with Toto, is almost inseparable from her friend Ozma, and keeps her crown at Ozma’s castle. She also has old friends including the Tin Woodman and knows Scraps. After visiting the Tin Woodman, Dorothy accidentally leaves the Emerald City while following Toto and meets Percy Vere. Learning that the Princess of Perhaps City is missing, she joins Percy and Toto in searching for her, intending to use Ozma’s magic picture. Their journey brings capture by a runaway road, the Tubbies, and the wild wash women. On Monday Mountain, Dorothy is imprisoned and compelled to wash, though Princess Pearl Borax protects her from actual harm. She refuses Pearl Borax’s proposed marriage to Percy and escapes with Percy and Toto. Recognized as Princess Dorothy of Oz, she helps Grampa’s army travel toward the Emerald City, endures being forced to play by the Play Fellows, and assists Grampa’s escape. At Maybe Mountain, she supports Tatters, organizes Abrog’s questioning, solves the backwards name “Gorba,” and identifies Tatters in the prophecy. She finds the cure for Urtha’s enchantment on the wizard’s bottle. Finally, she celebrates in Ragbad, dances the Ragbad Quadrille, and returns home with Toto after promising to visit Ozma in the Emerald City.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

9 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 9

Dorothy visits the Tin Woodman and then accidentally walks away from the Emerald City after Toto chases a baconfly. She meets Percy Vere, learns about the missing Princess of Perhaps City, and plans to use Ozma's magic picture to find her. Dorothy and Percy are captured by the runaway road but escape by grabbing a tree branch before entering a dark forest.

CHAPTER 14

Dorothy travels with Percy Vere and Toto toward the Emerald City. She prepares breakfast in a woodcutter’s cabin, leaves her gold ring as payment, and later helps Percy face the wild wash women. She explains that they are searching for a Princess, but the Tubbies carry her away with Percy.

CHAPTER 16

Dorothy is held prisoner on Monday Mountain and forced to work at the wash tubs with Percy Vere and the wild wash women tribe. She refuses to let Percy marry Pearl Borax and escapes with Percy and Toto through the gate in the running white fence.

CHAPTER 17

Dorothy meets Grampa's party and is recognized by King Fumbo as Princess Dorothy of Oz. She explains that she and Percy Vere have had a difficult journey and agrees to help search for the Princess of Perhaps City.

CHAPTER 18

Dorothy travels with Grampa's army toward the Emerald City and suggests knocking at the gate of the fenced park. The Play Fellows rush her to the swings and force her to play; she later helps Grampa escape by running beside him and carrying the red umbrella.

CHAPTER 19

Dorothy arrives with the Forgetful Poet, Percy Vere, and Grampa after following the trails to Maybe Mountain. She runs to Tatters when she sees him distressed and urges that he be allowed to explain what happened.

CHAPTER 20

Dorothy organizes the questioning of Abrog and identifies him as being at the bottom of the affair. She recognizes that “Gorba” is “Abrog” spelled backwards, interprets the prophecy, and realizes that Tatters is the youth it describes.

CHAPTER 21

Dorothy discovers the cure for enchantment on the wizard's bottle. She watches with Grampa, Percy Vere, and Peer Haps as the cure is applied to Urtha.

CHAPTER 22

Dorothy arrives in Ragbad with her companions and joins the celebrations there. She dances the Ragbad Quadrille, then returns home with Toto after promising to visit Ozma in the Emerald City.

Dorothy in The Lost King of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1925 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl, a Princess of Oz, and one of Ozma's closest friends. She lives in Oz, in a little apartment in Ozma's palace in the Emerald City, helps Ozma rule her subjects, and is known as the first little girl to discover Oz. She has Toto, a little white kitten, a stub of pencil, and silver dust she carries in her pocket. While visiting Perhaps City, Dorothy is missed by Ozma and the others. Traveling through the Winkie Country, she is blown to a road near Hollywood, where she rescues and names Humpy, a stuffed moving-picture dummy. Her sudden growth forces her to wish herself back to Oz with the silver dust, unintentionally bringing Humpy alive. When the dust is gone, she and Humpy must seek the Emerald City by road. Dorothy deciphers the Back Woods' language, passes the woodsmen, crosses a river with the Scooters, and travels with Kabumpo. She meets Snip and Tora, learns that Mombi plans to transform Ozma, and urges everyone onward. Dorothy is concerned about Mombi's mischief, helps investigate the empty palace and its sinking, and insists that Mombi undo the harm and restore Humpy before punishment. She shares clues from her journey, helps test the Soldier with the Green Whiskers, and learns the reason for her growth in California. At the end, she rides the Cowardly Lion in a grand procession and suggests using water against Mombi.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

10 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 8

Dorothy is visiting Perhaps City, and Ozma and the others are already longing for her return. The text identifies her as one of Ozma's closest friends and as the discoverer of the Scarecrow and Sir Hokus.

CHAPTER 10

Dorothy travels from Perhaps City through the Winkie Country, but a sudden blast carries her to a road marked thirty miles from Hollywood. She rescues a stuffed moving-picture dummy, names him Humpy, and tells him about Oz before her sudden growth forces her to wish herself back.

CHAPTER 11

Dorothy returns to Oz with Humpy after using wishing sand to wish herself back from America. She realizes that she also wished Humpy alive and later tries to wish them both to the Emerald City, but the sand is gone. She deciphers the Back Woods' language, obtains permission to pass, escapes the woodsmen, and continues toward the Emerald City.

CHAPTER 12

Dorothy plays catch with the Scooters using Humpy's crown and learns that they call unsailed travelers "Scoots." She explains that she is a Princess traveling to the Emerald City, and the Scooters carry her across the river. She later meets Kabumpo, explains Humpy's strange origin, and rides with both Humpy and Kabumpo toward the Emerald City.

CHAPTER 15

Dorothy meets Snip and Tora, eats breakfast with them, and tells them about her adventures since falling into California, including meeting the Scooters and Kabumpo. She becomes alarmed when she learns of Mombi's plan to transform Ozma and urges everyone to hurry to the Emerald City. Tora mends her torn dress with buttons supplied by Snip.

CHAPTER 16

Dorothy helps Snip and Tora after Kabumpo stops and recognizes Mombi and Pajuka when they emerge from the mountain. She doubts that Humpy can be a king but agrees that Mombi must restore him before being punished. Dorothy urges the group to proceed to the Emerald City so Ozma can settle the matter.

CHAPTER 17

Dorothy travels with the group to the Emerald City and worries that Mombi intends mischief. She helps ring the palace bell, discovers that the unlocked palace is empty, and leads the group inside before witnessing Mombi's spell and the palace's descent.

CHAPTER 19

Dorothy helps the others after the palace sinks and decides that Mombi must undo the damage. She allows Kabumpo to guard Mombi and later urges the Wizard to be careful when he attempts the transformation.

CHAPTER 20

Dorothy tells the group about her adventures with Snip, Pajuka, and the others, helping them compare the clues. She helps bring the Soldier with the Green Whiskers forward for testing and later learns why she changed size in California.

CHAPTER 21

Dorothy rides on the Cowardly Lion during the grand procession. She suggests putting Mombi out with water, as had been done with the other witches.

Dorothy in The Gnome King of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1927 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little Kansas girl and princess of Oz, Ozma’s favorite adviser on ruling and palace matters. She possesses a gold slipper and palace piano, captures Ruggedo’s magic belt, and plans Ozma’s homecoming party, overcoming Ruggedo’s mischief to help restore peace and welcome Ozma.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

1 section; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 17 Mystery In The Emerald City

Dorothy plans a party to welcome Ozma home, arranging a speech, games, dancing, food, decorations, invitations, and entertainment. She quarrels with the Scarecrow and the Cowardly Lion because of Ruggedo’s invisible pinching and pulling, but later helps restore peace. She welcomes Ozma when she returns and helps prepare the banquet.

Dorothy in Jack Pumpkinhead of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1929 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a little girl from the United States who lives in Emerald City, where she is Ozma's best friend and adviser and prefers life to America. With her dog Toto, she is captured in the garden, worries over the magic belt, reassures the Scarecrow, then returns with the rescued company and questions Ozma about Mogodore.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

2 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 17

Dorothy plays blind-man's buff in the garden and is captured and tied to a tree by Mogodore's soldiers. She worries that the magic belt may be found and tries to reassure the Scarecrow while they are prisoners.

CHAPTER 19

Dorothy returns to the banquet hall with the rescued company and asks Ozma what happened. She reports that the spearmen in the garden suddenly disappeared and asks how Mogodore became so tiny.

Dorothy in The Yellow Knight of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1930 · the work’s page · read it where Dorothy first appears

Abstract written by a model

Dorothy is a Princess of Oz from Kansas who lives in Ozma's palace with Ozma and the other girls, advises Ozma on important state matters, and has discovered the Scarecrow, Tin Woodman, Tik Tok, and Scraps. She agrees to join Sir Hokus's quest, helps search for the Comfortable Camel and inspect the missing magic picture, then accompanies Ozma to Corabia, recognizes the camel, and responds warmly to Sir Hokus and Princess Marygolden's story.

Narrative, section by section written by a model

3 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER ONE

Dorothy is spending the evening with Sir Hokus and others in her apartment and agrees to accompany him on the quest on Monday. She warns Scraps not to damage the furniture while celebrating and later discusses preparations for the journey.

CHAPTER SIX

Dorothy recognizes Tuzzle and explains that the camel must be the Comfortable Camel Sir Hokus brought to the Emerald City. She joins Ozma and the others in searching for the camel and later helps inspect the missing magic picture.

CHAPTER TWENTY

Dorothy accompanies Ozma to Corabia, recognizes the Comfortable Camel, and is astonished by Sir Hokus's transformation. She kisses Sir Hokus and Princess Marygolden after hearing their story.
